In <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox and Eastern Christianity</a> he often wears an <a href="/wiki/Orarion" title="Orarion">orarion</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Altar_server" title="Altar server">Altar Servers</a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external autonumber" href="http://totus2us.com/vocation/saints/st-stephen/">[1]</a>;<a href="/wiki/Acoma_Pueblo" title="Acoma Pueblo">Acoma</a> Indian <a href="/wiki/Pueblo" title="Pueblo">Pueblo</a>; <a href="/wiki/Bricklayer" title="Bricklayer">Bricklayers</a>; <a href="/wiki/Coffin" title="Coffin">casket</a> makers; <a href="/wiki/Cetona" title="Cetona">Cetona, Italy</a>; <a href="/wiki/Deacons" class="mw-redirect" title="Deacons">deacons</a>; <a href="/wiki/Headache" title="Headache">headaches</a>; <a href="/wiki/Horse" title="Horse">horses</a>; <a href="/wiki/Kessel,_Belgium" title="Kessel, Belgium">Kessel, Belgium</a>; <a href="/wiki/Masonry" title="Masonry">masons</a>; <a href="/wiki/Owensboro,_Kentucky" title="Owensboro, Kentucky">Owensboro, Kentucky</a>; <a href="/wiki/Passau" title="Passau">Passau, Germany</a>; <a href="/wiki/Kigali" title="Kigali">Kigali</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rwanda" title="Rwanda">Rwanda</a>; <a href="/wiki/Dodoma" title="Dodoma">Dodoma</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tanzania" title="Tanzania">Tanzania</a>; <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a>; <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_Srpska" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of Srpska">Republic of Srpska</a>; <a href="/wiki/Prato" title="Prato">Prato, Italy</a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external autonumber" href="http://saints.sqpn.com/saint-stephen-the-martyr/">[2]</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Stephen</b> (<a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a>: <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><span lang="grc">Στέφανος</span></span> <i>Stéphanos</i>, meaning "wreath, crown" and by extension "reward, honor, renown, fame", often given as a title rather than as a name; c. 5 – c. 34 AD) is traditionally venerated as the <a href="/wiki/Protomartyr" class="mw-redirect" title="Protomartyr">protomartyr</a> or first martyr of <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ssdca_1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ssdca-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>  According to the <a href="/wiki/Acts_of_the_Apostles" title="Acts of the Apostles">Acts of the Apostles</a>, he was a <a href="/wiki/Deacon" title="Deacon">deacon</a> in the early Church at <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a> who angered members of various <a href="/wiki/Synagogue" title="Synagogue">synagogues</a> by his teachings. Accused of <a href="/wiki/Blasphemy" title="Blasphemy">blasphemy</a> at his trial, he made a speech denouncing the Jewish authorities who were sitting in judgment on him<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A51%E2%80%9353&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:51–53&#93;</a></sup> and was then <a href="/wiki/Stoned_to_death" class="mw-redirect" title="Stoned to death">stoned to death</a>. <a href="/wiki/Saul_of_Tarsus" class="mw-redirect" title="Saul of Tarsus">Saul of Tarsus</a>, later known as Paul, a <a href="/wiki/Pharisee" class="mw-redirect" title="Pharisee">Pharisee</a> and Roman citizen who would later become a <a href="/wiki/Apostles_in_the_New_Testament" title="Apostles in the New Testament">Christian apostle</a>, participated in Stephen's <a href="/wiki/Martyrdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Martyrdom">martyrdom</a>.<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+22%3A20&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 22:20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The only source for information about Stephen is the <a href="/wiki/New_Testament" title="New Testament">New Testament</a> book of the Acts of the Apostles.<sup id="cite_ref-Newadvent_2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newadvent-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>  Stephen is mentioned in <a href="/wiki/Acts_6" title="Acts 6">Acts 6</a> as one of the Greek-speaking <a href="/wiki/Hellenistic_Jew" class="mw-redirect" title="Hellenistic Jew">Hellenistic Jews</a> selected to participate in a fairer distribution of welfare to the Greek-speaking widows.<sup id="cite_ref-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oriental_Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" title="Oriental Orthodox">Oriental Orthodox</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutheran</a> churches and the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_the_East" title="Church of the East">Church of the East</a> view Stephen as a <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saint</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Artistic representations often show Stephen with a crown symbolising martyrdom, three stones, <a href="/wiki/Palm_branch_(symbol)" class="mw-redirect" title="Palm branch (symbol)">martyr's palm frond</a>, <a href="/wiki/Censer" title="Censer">censer</a>, and often holding a miniature church building. Stephen is often shown as  a young, beardless man with a <a href="/wiki/Tonsure" title="Tonsure">tonsure</a>, wearing a deacon's <a href="/wiki/Vestments" class="mw-redirect" title="Vestments">vestments</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Background">Background</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Background">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Stephen is first mentioned in the <a href="/wiki/Acts_of_the_Apostles" title="Acts of the Apostles">Acts of the Apostles</a> as one of <a href="/wiki/Seven_deacons" class="mw-redirect" title="Seven deacons">seven deacons</a> appointed by the <a href="/wiki/Apostles_in_the_New_Testament" title="Apostles in the New Testament">Apostles</a> to distribute food and charitable aid to poorer members of the community in the early church. According to Orthodox belief, he was the eldest and is therefore called "archdeacon".<sup id="cite_ref-oca_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oca-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> As another deacon, Nicholas of Antioch, is specifically stated to have been a convert to <a href="/wiki/Judaism" title="Judaism">Judaism</a>, it may be assumed that Stephen was born Jewish, but nothing more is known about his previous life.<sup id="cite_ref-Newadvent_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newadvent-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> The reason for the appointment of the deacons is stated to have been dissatisfaction among <a href="/wiki/Hellenistic" class="mw-redirect" title="Hellenistic">Hellenistic</a> (that is, Greek-influenced and Greek-speaking) Jews that their widows were being slighted in preference to Hebraic ones in the daily distribution of food. Since the name "Stephanos" is Greek, it has been assumed that he was one of these Hellenistic Jews. Stephen is stated to have been full of faith and the Holy Spirit and to have performed miracles among the people.<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+6%3A5%2C+8&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 6:5, 8&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St_stephen.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/St_stephen.jpg/170px-St_stephen.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="273" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/St_stephen.jpg/255px-St_stephen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/2/20/St_stephen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="311" data-file-height="500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St_stephen.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Stoning of Saint Stephen</i>, altarpiece of San Giorgio Maggiore, Venice, by Jacopo &amp; Domenico <a href="/wiki/Tintoretto" title="Tintoretto">Tintoretto</a></div></div></div>
<p>It seems to have been among synagogues of Hellenistic Jews that he performed his teachings and "signs and wonders" since it is said that he aroused the opposition of the "<a href="/wiki/Synagogue_of_the_Libertines" title="Synagogue of the Libertines">Synagogue of the Freedmen</a>", and "of the <a href="/wiki/Cyrene_(city)" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyrene (city)">Cyrenians</a>, and of the <a href="/wiki/Alexandria" title="Alexandria">Alexandrians</a>, and of them that were of <a href="/wiki/Cilicia" title="Cilicia">Cilicia</a> and Asia".<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+6%3A9&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 6:9&#93;</a></sup> Members of these synagogues had challenged Stephen's teachings, but Stephen had bested them in debate. Furious at this humiliation, they suborned false testimony that Stephen had preached blasphemy against <a href="/wiki/Moses" title="Moses">Moses</a> and God. They dragged him to appear before the <a href="/wiki/Sanhedrin" title="Sanhedrin">Sanhedrin</a>, the supreme legal court of Jewish elders, accusing him of preaching against the <a href="/wiki/Second_Temple" title="Second Temple">Temple</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Torah" title="Torah">Mosaic Law</a>.<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+6%3A9%E2%80%9314&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 6:9–14&#93;</a></sup> Stephen is said to have been unperturbed, his face looking like "that of an angel".<sup id="cite_ref-Newadvent_2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newadvent-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Speech_to_Sanhedrin">Speech to Sanhedrin</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Speech to Sanhedrin">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In a long speech to the <a href="/wiki/Sanhedrin" title="Sanhedrin">Sanhedrin</a> comprising almost the whole of <a href="/wiki/Acts_7" title="Acts 7">Acts chapter 7</a>, Stephen presents his view of the history of <a href="/wiki/Israelites" title="Israelites">Israel</a>. The God of glory, he says, appeared to <a href="/wiki/Abraham" title="Abraham">Abraham</a> in <a href="/wiki/Mesopotamia" title="Mesopotamia">Mesopotamia</a>, thus establishing at the beginning of the speech one of its major themes, that God does not dwell only in one particular building (meaning the Temple).<sup id="cite_ref-Williams_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Stephen recounts the stories of the <a href="/wiki/Patriarchs_(Bible)" title="Patriarchs (Bible)">patriarchs</a> in some depth, and goes into even more detail in the case of <a href="/wiki/Moses" title="Moses">Moses</a>. God appeared to Moses in the burning bush,<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A30%E2%80%9332&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:30–32&#93;</a></sup> and inspired Moses to lead his people out of Egypt. Nevertheless, the Israelites turned to other gods.<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A39%E2%80%9343&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:39–43&#93;</a></sup> This establishes the second main theme of Stephen's speech, Israel's disobedience to God.<sup id="cite_ref-Williams_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Stephen faced two accusations: that he had declared that Jesus would destroy the Temple in Jerusalem and that he had changed the customs of Moses. <a href="/wiki/Pope_Benedict_XVI" title="Pope Benedict XVI">Pope Benedict XVI</a> stated in 2012 that St. Stephen appealed to the Jewish scriptures to prove how the laws of Moses were not subverted by Jesus but, instead, were being fulfilled.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> Stephen denounces his listeners<sup id="cite_ref-Williams_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> as "stiff-necked" people who, just as their ancestors had done, resist the Holy Spirit. "Was there ever a prophet your ancestors did not persecute? They even killed those who predicted the coming of the Righteous One. And now you have betrayed and murdered him."<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A51%E2%80%9353&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:51–53&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="The_stoning_of_Stephen">The stoning of Stephen</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: The stoning of Stephen">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:262px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG/260px-Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG" decoding="async" width="260" height="175" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG/390px-Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG/520px-Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG 2x" data-file-width="952" data-file-height="642" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Giovanni_Battista_Lucini_-_Martyrdom_of_St._Stephen.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Stoning of Saint Stephen</i> by <a href="/wiki/Giovanni_Battista_Lucini" title="Giovanni Battista Lucini">Giovanni Battista Lucini</a></div></div></div>
<p>Thus castigated, the account is that the crowd could contain their anger no longer.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> However, Stephen looked up and cried, "Look! I see heaven open and the <a href="/wiki/Son_of_man" title="Son of man">Son of Man</a> standing on the right hand of God!" He said that the recently resurrected Jesus was standing by the side of God.<sup id="cite_ref-Williams2_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams2-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A54&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:54&#93;</a></sup> The people from the crowd, who threw the first stones,<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Williams2_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams2-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> laid their coats down so as to be able to do this, at the feet of a "young man named Saul" (later identified as <a href="/wiki/Paul_the_Apostle" title="Paul the Apostle">Paul the Apostle</a>). Stephen prayed that the Lord would receive his spirit and his killers be forgiven, sank to his knees, and "fell asleep".<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A58%E2%80%9360&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:58–60&#93;</a></sup> Saul "approved of their killing him."<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+8%3A1&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 8:1&#93;</a></sup> In the aftermath of Stephen's death, the remaining disciples except for the apostles fled to distant lands, many to <a href="/wiki/Antioch" title="Antioch">Antioch</a>.<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+11%3A19%E2%80%9320&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 11:19–20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Location_of_the_martyrdom">Location of the martyrdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Location of the martyrdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The exact site of Stephen's stoning is not mentioned in Acts; instead there are two different traditions. One, claimed by noted French archaeologists <a href="/wiki/Louis-Hugues_Vincent" title="Louis-Hugues Vincent">Louis-Hugues Vincent</a> (1872–1960) and <a href="/wiki/F%C3%A9lix-Marie_Abel" title="Félix-Marie Abel">Félix-Marie Abel</a> (1878–1953) to be <a href="/wiki/Ancient_history" title="Ancient history">ancient</a>, places the event at Jerusalem's northern gate, while another one, dated by Vincent and Abel to the <a href="/wiki/Middle_Ages" title="Middle Ages">Middle Ages</a> and no earlier than the 12th century, locates it at the eastern gate.<sup id="cite_ref-Corpus_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Corpus-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Views_of_Stephen.27s_speech"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Views_of_Stephen's_speech">Views of Stephen's speech</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Views of Stephen&#039;s speech">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg/170px-Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="230" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg/255px-Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg/340px-Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1422" data-file-height="1920" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Luis_de_Morales_-_St_Stephen.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i>Saint Stephen</i> by <a href="/wiki/Luis_de_Morales" title="Luis de Morales">Luis de Morales</a></div></div></div>
<p>Of the numerous speeches in Acts of the Apostles, Stephen's speech to the Sanhedrin is the longest.<sup id="cite_ref-Koivisto_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Koivisto-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> To the objection that it seems unlikely that such a long speech could be reproduced in the text of Acts exactly as it was delivered, some Biblical scholars have replied that Stephen's speech shows a distinctive personality behind it.<sup id="cite_ref-Williams_6-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There are at least five places where Stephen's re-telling of the stories of Israelite history diverges from the scriptures where these stories originated; for instance, Stephen says that Jacob's tomb was in <a href="/wiki/Shechem" title="Shechem">Shechem</a>,<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+7%3A16&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 7:16&#93;</a></sup> but Genesis 50:13<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Genesis+50%3A13&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Genesis 50:13&#93;</a></sup> says Jacob's body was carried and buried in a cave in Machpelah at <a href="/wiki/Hebron" title="Hebron">Hebron</a>.<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+8%3A1&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 8:1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Williams_6-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>  Some theologians argue that these may not be discrepancies, but rather a condensing of historical events for people who were already familiar with them.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> That Jacob's body was carried to a final resting place in Shechem is not recorded in Genesis, though it does not exclude the possibility that his bones were transferred to Shechem for a final burial place, as was done with the bones of Jacob's son Joseph, as described in <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Joshua+24%3A32&amp;version=NIV">Joshua 24:32</a> Other scholars consider them as errors. Still others interpret them as deliberate choices making theological points.<sup id="cite_ref-Koivisto_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Koivisto-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>  Another possibility is that the discrepancies come from an ancient Jewish tradition which was not included in the scriptures or may have been popular among people of Jerusalem who were not scribes.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Numerous parallels between the accounts of Stephen in Acts and the <a href="/wiki/Jesus" title="Jesus">Jesus</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Gospel" title="Gospel">Gospels</a> – they both perform miracles, they are both tried by the Sanhedrin, they both pray for forgiveness for their killers, for instance – have led to suspicions that the author of Acts has emphasised – in order to show the recipient that people become holy when they follow the example of Christ – or invented some (or all) of these.<sup id="cite_ref-Williams2_9-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams2-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The criticism of traditional Jewish belief and practice in Stephen's speech is very strong – when he says God does not live in a dwelling "made by human hands", referring to the Temple, he is using an expression often employed by Biblical texts to describe idols.<sup id="cite_ref-Williams_6-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Williams-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some people have laid the charge of anti-Judaism against the speech, for instance the priest and scholar of comparative religion <a href="/wiki/S._G._F._Brandon" title="S. G. F. Brandon">S. G. F. Brandon</a>, who states: "The anti-Jewish polemic of this speech reflects the attitude of the author of Acts."<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Commentary">Commentary</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Commentary">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p><a href="/wiki/Friedrich_Justus_Knecht" class="mw-redirect" title="Friedrich Justus Knecht">Friedrich Justus Knecht</a> lists the similarities of the martyrdom of Stephen to Jesus' death on the cross:
</p>
<blockquote>
<p>1. Our Blessed Lord was sentenced to death on the charge of blasphemy, because He had affirmed on oath: “I am the Son of the living God, and hereafter you will see the Son of Man sitting at the right hand of God.” In the same manner Stephen was stoned on the assumption that he was a blasphemer, and because he professed his belief in the Divinity of Jesus, and said: “I see heaven open, and Jesus standing at the right hand of God.” 2. Both our Blessed Lord and St. Stephen were treated as outcasts, and put to death outside the city. 3. Both, when dying, prayed for their enemies: “Father, forgive them, for they know not what they do.” — “Lay not this sin to their charge.” 4. Both, before dying, commended their souls to God: “Father, into Thy hands I commend My spirit.” — “Lord Jesus, receive my soul!” <sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
</blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Tomb_and_relics_of_Stephen">Tomb and relics of Stephen</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Tomb and relics of Stephen">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG/220px-Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG/330px-Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG/440px-Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Place_of_the_stoning_of_St._Stephen_in_Jerusalem.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Reputed site of the stoning of Stephen, Greek Orthodox Church of St Stephen, <a href="/wiki/Kidron_Valley" title="Kidron Valley">Kidron Valley</a>, Jerusalem</div></div></div>
<p>Acts 8:2<sup class="nourlexpansion reference"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.biblegateway.com/passage/?search=Acts+8%3A2&amp;version=NRSV">&#91;Acts 8:2&#93;</a></sup> says "Godly men buried Stephen and mourned deeply for him," but the location where he was buried is not specified.
</p><p>In 415, a priest named <a href="/w/index.php?title=Fr._Lucian&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Fr. Lucian (page does not exist)">Lucian</a> purportedly had a dream that revealed the location of Stephen's remains at <a href="/wiki/Beit_Jimal" title="Beit Jimal">Beit Jimal</a>. After that, the <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">relics</a> of the protomartyr were taken in procession to the <a href="/wiki/Dormition_Abbey" class="mw-redirect" title="Dormition Abbey">Church of Hagia Sion</a> on 26 December 415, making it the date for the feast of Saint Stephen. In 439, the relics were translated to a new church north of the Damascus Gate built by the empress <a href="/wiki/Aelia_Eudocia" title="Aelia Eudocia">Aelia Eudocia</a> in honor of Saint Stephen. This church was destroyed in the 12th century. A 20th-century French Catholic church, Saint-Étienne, was built in its place, while another, the Greek Orthodox Church of St Stephen, was built outside the eastern gate of the city,<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> which a second tradition holds to be the site of his martyrdom, rather than the northern location outside Damascus Gate (for the two traditions see <a href="#Location_of_the_martyrdom">here</a>).
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Crusades" title="Crusades">Crusaders</a> initially called the main northern gate of Jerusalem "Saint Stephen's Gate" (in Latin, <i>Porta Sancti Stephani</i>), highlighting its proximity to the site of martyrdom of Saint Stephen, marked by the church and monastery built by Empress Eudocia.<sup id="cite_ref-Boasp53_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Boasp53-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> A different tradition is documented from the end of the Crusader period, after the disappearance of the Byzantine church: as Christian pilgrims were prohibited from approaching the militarily exposed northern city wall, the name "Saint Stephen's Gate" was transferred to the still accessible eastern gate, which bears this name until this day.<sup id="cite_ref-MurphyOConnor_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MurphyOConnor-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The relics of the protomartyr were later translated to Rome by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pelagius_II" title="Pope Pelagius II">Pope Pelagius II</a> during the construction of the <a href="/wiki/Basilica" title="Basilica">basilica</a> of <a href="/wiki/San_Lorenzo_fuori_le_mura" title="San Lorenzo fuori le mura">San Lorenzo fuori le Mura</a>. They were interred alongside the relics of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Lawrence" title="Saint Lawrence">Saint Lawrence</a>, whose tomb is enshrined within the church. According to the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Legend" title="Golden Legend">Golden Legend</a>, the relics of Lawrence moved miraculously to one side to make room for those of Stephen.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Regalia" title="Imperial Regalia">Imperial Regalia</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a> includes a relic known as <a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Purse" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Purse">St. Stephen's Purse</a> which is an elaborate gold and jewel-encrusted box believed to contain soil soaked with the blood of St. Stephen. The reliquary is likely a 9th-century creation.
</p><p>In his book <i><a href="/wiki/The_City_of_God" title="The City of God">The City of God</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Augustine_of_Hippo" title="Augustine of Hippo">Augustine of Hippo</a> describes the many miracles that occurred when part of the relics of Saint Stephen were brought to Africa.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Part of the right arm of Saint Stephen is enshrined at <a href="/wiki/Trinity_Lavra_of_St._Sergius" title="Trinity Lavra of St. Sergius">Trinity Lavra of St. Sergius</a> in Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Accuracy_dispute#Disputed_statement" title="Wikipedia:Accuracy dispute"><span title="The material near this tag is possibly inaccurate or nonfactual. (August 2016)">dubious</span></a>&#32;<span class="metadata"> &#8211; <a href="/wiki/Talk:Saint_Stephen#Weak_source,_a_blog,_be_it_by_a_cleric._Many_local_traditions_are_not_recognised_beyond_the_district_or_country_borders._It&#39;s_not_science_anyhow,_but_does_the_set_of_relics_in_Rome_lack_the_right_hand_bones?_I.e.,_what_do_the_Catholics_say?" title="Talk:Saint Stephen">discuss</a></span></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Saint_Stephen.27s_Day"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Saint_Stephen's_Day">Saint Stephen's Day</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Saint Stephen&#039;s Day">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Saint_Stephen%27s_Day" title="Saint Stephen&#39;s Day">Saint Stephen's Day</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_holidays">Public holidays</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Public holidays">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In <a href="/wiki/Western_Christianity" title="Western Christianity">Western Christianity</a>, 26 December is called "<a href="/wiki/Saint_Stephen%27s_Day" title="Saint Stephen&#39;s Day">Saint Stephen's Day</a>", the "Feast of Stephen" mentioned in the English <a href="/wiki/Christmas_carol" title="Christmas carol">Christmas carol</a> "<a href="/wiki/Good_King_Wenceslas" title="Good King Wenceslas">Good King Wenceslas</a>". It is a <a href="/wiki/Public_holiday" title="Public holiday">public holiday</a> in many nations that are of historic Catholic, Anglican and Lutheran traditions, including Austria, Croatia, the <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_the_Czech_Republic" title="Public holidays in the Czech Republic">Czech Republic</a>, Hungary, Ireland, Luxembourg, Slovakia, Poland, Italy, Germany, Norway, Sweden, Denmark, Finland, Catalonia and the Balearic Isles. In Australia, New Zealand, Canada and the United Kingdom, the day is celebrated as "<a href="/wiki/Boxing_Day" title="Boxing Day">Boxing Day</a>".
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Western_Christianity">Western Christianity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Western Christianity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the current norms for the <a href="/wiki/Liturgy" title="Liturgy">liturgy</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a>, the feast is celebrated at the <a href="/wiki/Mass_(liturgy)" title="Mass (liturgy)">Eucharist</a>, but, for the <a href="/wiki/Liturgy_of_the_Hours" title="Liturgy of the Hours">Liturgy of the Hours</a>, is restricted to the Hours during the day, with <a href="/wiki/Vespers" title="Vespers">Evening Prayer</a> being reserved to the celebration of the <a href="/wiki/Octave_(liturgical)" class="mw-redirect" title="Octave (liturgical)">Octave</a> of <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a>. Historically, the "Invention of the Relics of Saint Stephen" (i.e., their reputed discovery) was commemorated on 3 August.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> The feasts of both 26 December and 3 August have been used in dating clauses in historical documents produced in England.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Stephen is <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints_(Church_of_England)" title="Calendar of saints (Church of England)">remembered</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> with a <a href="/wiki/Festival_(Anglicanism)" title="Festival (Anglicanism)">Festival</a> on <a href="/wiki/December_26" title="December 26">26 December</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Eastern_Christianity">Eastern Christianity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Eastern Christianity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg/170px-Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="297" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg/255px-Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2c/Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg/340px-Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg 2x" data-file-width="627" data-file-height="1094" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Stephen_11cent.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Byzantine icon, 11th century</div></div></div>
<p>In the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a>, those <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Catholic_Churches" title="Eastern Catholic Churches">Eastern Catholic Churches</a> which follow the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Rite" title="Byzantine Rite">Byzantine Rite</a>, and in Oriental Orthodox Churches (e.g., Coptic, Syrian, Malankara) Saint Stephen's feast day is celebrated on 27 December, due to the celebration of the <a href="/wiki/Synaxis" title="Synaxis">Synaxis</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Theotokos" title="Theotokos">Theotokos</a> on the 26 December. This also has the effect of pushing the Feast of the <a href="/wiki/Massacre_of_the_Innocents" title="Massacre of the Innocents">Holy Innocents</a> to the 29 December. This day is also called the "Third Day of the Nativity" because it is the third day of the Christmas season.
</p><p>Some Orthodox churches, particularly in the west, follow a modified Julian calendar that places date names identically with the standard Gregorian calendar of widespread civil usage. In those churches, then, the date the feast is observed is generally known as 27 December. However, other Orthodox churches, including the Oriental Orthodox, continue to use the original Julian calendar. Throughout the 21st century, 27 December Julian will continue to fall on 9 January in the Gregorian calendar, and that is the date on which they observe the feast.
</p><p>Saint Stephen is also <a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">commemorated</a> on 4 January (<a href="/wiki/Synaxis" title="Synaxis">Synaxis</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Seventy_apostles" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventy apostles">Seventy Apostles</a>) in the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Translation_(relic)" title="Translation (relic)">Uncovering of his relics</a> (relics of the <a href="/wiki/Saint" title="Saint">saints</a>: <a href="/wiki/Nicodemus" title="Nicodemus">Nicodemus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gamaliel" title="Gamaliel">Gamaliel</a> and Abibas son of Gamaliel were also found in Saint Stephen's tomb) took place in 415, Gamaliel appeared to <a href="/w/index.php?title=%D0%9B%D1%83%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B0%D0%BD_(%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80_%D0%9A%D0%B0%D1%84%D0%B0%D1%80_%D0%93%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8B)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Лукиан (пресвитер Кафар Гамалы) (page does not exist)">presbyter Lucian</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%9B%D1%83%D0%BA%D0%B8%D0%B0%D0%BD_(%D0%BF%D1%80%D0%B5%D1%81%D0%B2%D0%B8%D1%82%D0%B5%D1%80_%D0%9A%D0%B0%D1%84%D0%B0%D1%80_%D0%93%D0%B0%D0%BC%D0%B0%D0%BB%D1%8B)" class="extiw" title="ru:Лукиан (пресвитер Кафар Гамалы)">ru</a>&#93;</span> and he told him to go to <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a> and inform <a href="/wiki/John_II_(bishop_of_Jerusalem)" title="John II (bishop of Jerusalem)">Bishop John</a> about <a href="/wiki/Relic" title="Relic">relics</a> of Saint Stephen.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/John_II_(bishop_of_Jerusalem)" title="John II (bishop of Jerusalem)">Bishop John II</a> with bishops Eusthia (from <a href="/wiki/Sebastia,_Nablus" title="Sebastia, Nablus">Sebastia</a>) and Eleutherius (from Jericho) came to the tomb in <a href="/wiki/Beit_Jimal" title="Beit Jimal">Beit Jimal</a> and translated relics to Jerusalem, this event is commemorated on 15 September.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 428 (when <a href="/wiki/Theodosius_II" title="Theodosius II">Saint Theodosius II the Younger</a> <a href="/wiki/Roman_emperor" title="Roman emperor">Roman Emperor</a>) relics of saint: Stephen, <a href="/wiki/Nicodemus" title="Nicodemus">Nicodemus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gamaliel" title="Gamaliel">Gamaliel</a> and Abibas were translated from <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a> to <a href="/wiki/Constantinople" title="Constantinople">Constantinople</a> and relics have been placed in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Lawrence" title="Saint Lawrence">Saint Lawrence</a> church, and after preparations were made relics were moved to specially prepared Saint Stephen church in Constantinople, this event took place on 2 August.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:0_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Armenian_Liturgy">Armenian Liturgy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Armenian Liturgy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In the Armenian Apostolic and Armenian Catholic Churches, Saint Stephen's Day falls on 25 December – the day on which the feast of the Nativity of Jesus (Christmas) falls in all other churches. This is because the Armenian churches maintain the decree of Constantine, which stipulated that the Nativity and Theophany of Jesus were to be celebrated on 6 January. In dioceses of the Armenian Church which use the Julian Calendar, Saint Stephen's Day falls on 7 January and Nativity/Theophany on 19 January (for the remainder of the 21st century Julian).
</p><p>In the eucharistic celebration on this feast day, it is traditional for all deacons serving at the altar to wear a liturgical crown (Armenian: խոյր <i>khooyr</i>), which is one of the vestments worn only by priests on all other days of the year, the crown being in this instance a symbol of martyrdom.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Commemorative_places">Commemorative places</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Commemorative places">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St-Stepanous-02.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/St-Stepanous-02.jpg/220px-St-Stepanous-02.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/St-Stepanous-02.jpg/330px-St-Stepanous-02.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c8/St-Stepanous-02.jpg/440px-St-Stepanous-02.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St-Stepanous-02.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Saint_Stepanos_Monastery" title="Saint Stepanos Monastery">Saint Stephen Armenian Monastery</a> of the 9th century near Jolfa, Iran</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:StStephen_GiacomoCavedone.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/StStephen_GiacomoCavedone.jpg/220px-StStephen_GiacomoCavedone.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="328" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/4/49/StStephen_GiacomoCavedone.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="268" data-file-height="400" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:StStephen_GiacomoCavedone.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Saint Stephen holding a <a href="/wiki/Gospel_Book" title="Gospel Book">Gospel Book</a> in a 1601 painting by <a href="/wiki/Giacomo_Cavedone" title="Giacomo Cavedone">Giacomo Cavedone</a>.</div></div></div>
<dl><dd><i><b>See also</b>: <a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Cathedral_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Cathedral (disambiguation)">St. Stephen's Cathedral</a>, <a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Church_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Church (disambiguation)">St. Stephen's Church</a></i></dd></dl>
<p>Many churches and other places commemorate Stephen. Among the most notable are the two sites in Jerusalem held by different traditions to be the place of his martyrdom, the <a href="/wiki/Salesian" class="mw-redirect" title="Salesian">Salesian</a> monastery of <a href="/wiki/Beit_Jimal" title="Beit Jimal">Beit Jimal</a> in <a href="/wiki/Israel" title="Israel">Israel</a> held to be the place where his remains were miraculously found, and the church of <a href="/wiki/San_Lorenzo_fuori_le_Mura" class="mw-redirect" title="San Lorenzo fuori le Mura">San Lorenzo fuori le Mura</a> in Rome, where the saint's remains are said to be buried.
</p><p>Important churches and sites dedicated to Saint Stephen are:
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Armenian_churches">Armenian churches</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Armenian churches">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Lmbatavank" title="Lmbatavank">Saint Stephen Church of Lmbat</a> of the 7th century, near the town of <a href="/wiki/Artik" title="Artik">Artik</a>, <a href="/wiki/Armenia" title="Armenia">Armenia</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Saint_Stepanos_Monastery" title="Saint Stepanos Monastery">Saint Stephen Armenian Monastery</a> of the 9th century, near the city of <a href="/wiki/Jolfa,_Iran_(city)" title="Jolfa, Iran (city)">Jolfa</a>, northwestern <a href="/wiki/Iran" title="Iran">Iran</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stepanos_Church" title="St. Stepanos Church">St. Stepanos Armenian Church</a> of <a href="/wiki/Izmir" class="mw-redirect" title="Izmir">Izmir</a>, <a href="/wiki/Turkey" title="Turkey">Turkey</a>, built in 1863 and destroyed in September 1922 during the <a href="/wiki/Great_Fire_of_Smyrna" class="mw-redirect" title="Great Fire of Smyrna">Catastrophe of Smyrna</a>.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Australia">Australia</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Australia">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Cathedral_of_St_Stephen,_Brisbane" title="Cathedral of St Stephen, Brisbane">St Stephen's Cathedral</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brisbane" title="Brisbane">Brisbane</a>, <a href="/wiki/Queensland" title="Queensland">Queensland</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a> – is the primary Catholic place of worship in the archdiocese of Brisbane.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Austria">Austria</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Austria">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Stephansdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Stephansdom">Stephansdom</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a>, <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a> – the Cathedral of St. Stephen, founded 1147 and seat of the <a href="/wiki/Archbishop_of_Vienna" title="Archbishop of Vienna">Archbishop of Vienna</a>. Symbol of the city of Vienna and of Austria, has the tallest spire in Austria and is the "centerpiece of Vienna".<sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="France">France</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: France">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Saint_%C3%89tienne" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Étienne">Saint Étienne</a>, France, and <a href="/wiki/Saint_Etienne_(disambiguation)" class="mw-redirect mw-disambig" title="Saint Etienne (disambiguation)">numerous other places named Saint Étienne</a> in the French-speaking world (Étienne is the French form of Stephen).</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="India">India</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: India">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/w/index.php?title=St._Stephen%27s_School_(ICSE/ISC),_Sonarpur,_Kolkata&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="St. Stephen&#39;s School (ICSE/ISC), Sonarpur, Kolkata (page does not exist)">St. Stephen's School (ICSE/ISC), Sonarpur, Kolkata</a>, Under <a href="/wiki/Church_of_North_India" title="Church of North India">Church of North India</a> in <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> Principal, Hebron Larruna Peters.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Church,_Kombuthurai" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Church, Kombuthurai">St. Stephen's Church, Kombuthurai</a>, built by <a href="/wiki/Francis_Xavier" title="Francis Xavier">Francis Xavier</a> in <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a> in 1542.</li>
<li>St. Stephen's knanaya Catholic Forane Church, Uzhavoor, kottayam, built 1631.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Kudassanad#St.Stephen&#39;s_Orthodox_Cathedral" title="Kudassanad">St. Stephen's Orthodox Cathedral, Kudassanad, Pandalam, Kerala</a> the first Orthodox Cathedral of Saint Stephen in India.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_College,_Delhi" title="St. Stephen&#39;s College, Delhi">St. Stephen's College, Delhi</a>.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Church,_Delhi" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Church, Delhi">St. Stephen's Church, Delhi</a> and St. Stephen's Hospital, Delhi.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_School,_Chandigarh" title="St. Stephen&#39;s School, Chandigarh">St. Stephen's School, Chandigarh</a> founded in 1986.</li>
<li>St. Stephen's Church, Thope, is one of the parishes of the first diocese of India, Kollam. It is 216 years old and the patron of this parish is St. Stephen, the first Martyr of the Church and it is situated beside Kollam Beach.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_Estevam#St._Estevam_Church" title="St Estevam">St Stephen Church</a> in <a href="/wiki/St_Estevam" title="St Estevam">Santo Estêvão</a>, <a href="/wiki/Goa" title="Goa">Goa</a>, <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ireland">Ireland</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Ireland">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/St_Stephen%27s_Green" title="St Stephen&#39;s Green">St Stephen's Green</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dublin" title="Dublin">Dublin</a>. The largest of Dublin's Georgian squares and itself named after a former leper hospital near the site.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Church_of_St._Stephen,_Tyrrellspass&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Church of St. Stephen, Tyrrellspass (page does not exist)">Church of St. Stephen, Tyrrellspass</a>, located in <a href="/wiki/Tyrrellspass" title="Tyrrellspass">Tyrrellspass</a>, <a href="/wiki/County_Westmeath" title="County Westmeath">County Westmeath</a>.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Italy">Italy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Italy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>Rome – <a href="/wiki/Santo_Stefano_al_Monte_Celio" title="Santo Stefano al Monte Celio">Santo Stefano Rotondo</a>, a church built under the commission of Constantine I on the ruins of the Caelian Hill of Rome. Built in the 5th Century, it is the first church in Rome to have a circular floor plan, instead of the traditional <a href="/wiki/Greek_Cross" class="mw-redirect" title="Greek Cross">Greek</a> or <a href="/wiki/Latin_cross" title="Latin cross">Latin cross</a> designs.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/San_Lorenzo_fuori_le_Mura" class="mw-redirect" title="San Lorenzo fuori le Mura">San Lorenzo fuori le Mura</a>, where Saint Stephen is said to be interred together with Saint Lawrence in the crypt, under the high altar.</li>
<li>Vatican City – <a href="/wiki/Santo_Stefano_degli_Abissini" title="Santo Stefano degli Abissini">Santo Stefano degli Abissini</a>, Coptic Christian church in Vatican City, that is also the <a href="/wiki/National_churches_in_Rome" title="National churches in Rome">National Church of Ethiopia in Rome</a>.</li>
<li>Rome – <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Saint_Paul_Outside_the_Walls" title="Basilica of Saint Paul Outside the Walls">Basilica Papale di San Paolo fuori le Mura</a>, a side chapel to St. Stephen is about a stone's throw from the tomb of St. Paul.</li>
<li>Milan – <a href="/wiki/Basilica_di_Santo_Stefano_Maggiore" title="Basilica di Santo Stefano Maggiore">Basilica di Santo Stefano Maggiore</a>, a baroque church built in the fifth century and originally dedicated to both Saint Stephen and <a href="/wiki/Zechariah_(New_Testament_figure)" title="Zechariah (New Testament figure)">Saint Zecheriah</a>.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Holy_Land">Holy Land</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Holy Land">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Basilica,_Jerusalem" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Basilica, Jerusalem">St. Stephen's Basilica, Jerusalem</a>, in French <b>Saint-Étienne</b>, at the traditional place of St Stephen's martyrdom; modern church over ruins of Byzantine 5th-century predecessor.</li>
<li><b>St. Stephan's Gate</b>, the Christian name of one of the city gates of the <a href="/wiki/Old_City_(Jerusalem)" class="mw-redirect" title="Old City (Jerusalem)">Old City</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a>, also known as the "<a href="/wiki/The_Lions%27_Gate" class="mw-redirect" title="The Lions&#39; Gate">Lions' Gate</a>". A post-Byzantine tradition holds that Stephen's stoning occurred there, while an older tradition connects the martyrdom to the Damascus Gate, where a <a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Basilica,_Jerusalem" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Basilica, Jerusalem">church</a> and large monastic complex dedicated to Saint Stephen was built in the 5th century (see <a href="#Location_of_the_martyrdom">above</a>). A modern Greek Orthodox Church of Saint Stephen stands a short distance from Lions' Gate.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="United_Kingdom">United Kingdom</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: United Kingdom">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/St_Stephen%27s_Chapel" title="St Stephen&#39;s Chapel">St Stephen's Chapel</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Palace_of_Westminster" title="Palace of Westminster">Palace of Westminster</a>, London, was originally built in the reign of <a href="/wiki/Henry_III_of_England" title="Henry III of England">Henry III of England</a>; it became the first site of the debating chamber of the <a href="/wiki/British_House_of_Commons" class="mw-redirect" title="British House of Commons">British House of Commons</a>. The tower that houses <a href="/wiki/Big_Ben" title="Big Ben">Big Ben</a>, that was properly called The Clock Tower, was referred to as St Stephen's Tower by Victorian journalists.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The Clock Tower was renamed Elizabeth Tower to commemorate the <a href="/wiki/Diamond_Jubilee_of_Elizabeth_II" title="Diamond Jubilee of Elizabeth II">Diamond Jubilee of Elizabeth II</a> in 2013. St Stephen's Tower is the smaller tower in the middle of the building.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_Stephen%27s_House,_Oxford" title="St Stephen&#39;s House, Oxford">St Stephen's House, Oxford</a> – a <a href="/wiki/Permanent_private_hall" title="Permanent private hall">permanent private hall</a> of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Oxford" title="University of Oxford">University of Oxford</a> and <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a> theological college.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_Stephen%27s_Church,_Bristol" title="St Stephen&#39;s Church, Bristol">St Stephen's Church, Bristol</a> – the first<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2013)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> city church built outside the walls c. 1250, rebuilt c. 1430–1490.</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=St_Stephen%27s,_Sneinton,_Nottingham&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="St Stephen&#39;s, Sneinton, Nottingham (page does not exist)">St Stephen's, Sneinton, Nottingham</a> - Has strong links to William Booth and The Salvation Army. The parents of D.H. Lawrence married in the church on 27 December 1875.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St_Stephen_Walbrook" title="St Stephen Walbrook">St Stephen's Walbrook</a>, City of London - first recorded in C11 and rebuilt to Wren's design after the Great Fire</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="United_States">United States</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: United States">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>St. Stephen the martyr church, Renton, Washington <sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>St. Stephen Parish in Portland, Oregon.</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Catholic_Church_(Cleveland,_Ohio)" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Catholic Church (Cleveland, Ohio)">St. Stephen Church</a> in Cleveland, Ohio.</li>
<li>St. Stephen Protomartyr Catholic Church and Parish in St. Louis, Missouri.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/St._Stephen%27s_Church_(Boston,_Massachusetts)" title="St. Stephen&#39;s Church (Boston, Massachusetts)">St. Stephen's Church</a> in Boston, Massachusetts.</li>
<li>St. Stephen Catholic Church in Cincinnati, Ohio.</li>
<li>St. Stephen's Church in Providence, RI.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>St. Stephen the Martyr Church, Omaha, Nebraska <sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_associations">Other associations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Stephen&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Other associations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li>In the Catholic Church, the <a href="/wiki/Guild_of_St._Stephen" title="Guild of St. Stephen">Guild of St. Stephen</a> is an international association of <a href="/wiki/Altar_server" title="Altar server">altar servers</a> whose aim is to promote "highest standards of serving at the Church's liturgy".<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>Saint Stephen is one of the sculptures on the side of the <a href="/wiki/Orsanmichele" title="Orsanmichele">Orsanmichele</a> in <a href="/wiki/Florence,_Italy" class="mw-redirect" title="Florence, Italy">Florence</a>. Saint Stephen is the patron saint of the <a href="/wiki/Guilds_of_Florence" title="Guilds of Florence">wool guild</a>.</li>
<li>Saint Stephen is featured as the eponymous subject of a song by the  <a href="/wiki/Grateful_Dead" title="Grateful Dead">Grateful Dead</a>.</li></ul>

<li id="cite_note-11"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-11">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFUnger2006" class="citation book cs1"><a href="/wiki/Merrill_Unger" title="Merrill Unger">Unger, Merrill F.</a> (2006) [1957].  Harrison, R. K. (ed.). <i>The New Unger's Bible Dictionary</i>. Chicago: Moody Publishers. Antioch. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-8024-9066-7" title="Special:BookSources/978-0-8024-9066-7"><bdi>978-0-8024-9066-7</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=The+New+Unger%27s+Bible+Dictionary&amp;rft.place=Chicago&amp;rft.pages=Antioch&amp;rft.pub=Moody+Publishers&amp;rft.date=2006&amp;rft.isbn=978-0-8024-9066-7&amp;rft.aulast=Unger&amp;rft.aufirst=Merrill+F.&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASaint+Stephen" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-Corpus-12"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-Corpus_12-0">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFHannah_M._CottonLeah_Di_SegniWerner_EckBenjamin_Isaac2012" class="citation book cs1">Hannah M. Cotton; Leah Di Segni; Werner Eck;  et&#160;al., eds. (2012). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://books.google.com/books?id=2cfmBQAAQBAJ&amp;q=%22saint+stephen%22+%22stoning%22+%22Damascus+Gate%22+%22Lions+Gate%22&amp;pg=PA275"><i>Jerusalem, Part 2: 705–1120</i></a>. Corpus Inscriptionum Iudeae/Palaestinae. Vol.&#160;1. De Gruyter. p.&#160;275. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-3-11-025188-3" title="Special:BookSources/978-3-11-025188-3"><bdi>978-3-11-025188-3</bdi></a><span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">31 August</span> 2016</span>. <q>.... St. Stephen's Gate (Lions' gate; Bab Sitti Mariam). The gate owes its name to a tradition according to which Stephen the Deacon, the first martyr, was stoned on this spot. At the beginning of the 20 c. the Greek Orthodox Patriarchy built a church dedicated to the Protomartyr in their property in front of the gate, in an endeavour to pinpoint the tradition of the site, which was falling into oblivion following the construction of the Dominican church and monastery on the site of the Eudocian church of St. Stephen north of <a href="/wiki/Damascus_Gate" title="Damascus Gate">Damascus Gate</a>. The Greek builders went so far as to maintain that, in digging the foundations of the new church, they had found a broken lintel with an engraved invocation to Saint Stephen, but their claim, accepted by Macalister and Vailhé, was promptly disproved by Vincent, who was able to show that the lintel came in fact from Beersheba. Vincent and Abel maintained that the tradition about Stephen's stoning at the eastern gate of Jerusalem was not earlier than the 12 c., while the tradition pointing to the northern gate was ancient. .... J. Milik .... suggested that all the tombstones discovered in this area belonged to the cemetery of the Probatica.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Jerusalem%2C+Part+2%3A+705%E2%80%931120&amp;rft.series=Corpus+Inscriptionum+Iudeae%2FPalaestinae&amp;rft.pages=275&amp;rft.pub=De+Gruyter&amp;rft.date=2012&amp;rft.isbn=978-3-11-025188-3&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbooks.google.com%2Fbooks%3Fid%3D2cfmBQAAQBAJ%26q%3D%2522saint%2Bstephen%2522%2B%2522stoning%2522%2B%2522Damascus%2BGate%2522%2B%2522Lions%2BGate%2522%26pg%3DPA275&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ASaint+Stephen" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
